Mostly I just feel odd about it, like, why did I buy all those sweets when I didn’t even really want them? I came in because my eating’s been all turned around for months now—some days I just pick at toast and coffee, and other days I’m standing in the bakery aisle buying things I don’t even enjoy. Then afterward I sort of sit there feeling foolish.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: I guess it feels like that sometimes, yes… not exactly like I’m hungry and choosing it. I used to pass that aisle when my kids were little—well, not kids now, obviously—and it was just normal shopping. I used to be more normal about meals, I think… well, my mother always said breakfast mattered, but lately it’s just been strange.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure about the number, really. I don’t weigh myself much. It’s more that my eating has gotten strange for months… like some days I barely pick at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even want. I used to be more regular with meals, or I think I was.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not exactly. I mean, I don’t like how I look much, but I’m not sitting there counting every pound or anything. It’s more like food just feels… off. My sister used to worry about her jeans getting tight after holidays, and I remember thinking that was such a normal thing to care about. I don’t know if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, like within an hour?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, I guess. Not like… I don’t know what counts as “large,” but there are days I’ll go to the bakery aisle and buy sweets, and then I’m eating them even though I don’t really enjoy them. Other days I barely have toast and coffee. It’s all just been turned around for months now… I used to be more normal with meals, I think.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel guilty after eating?\n\nPatient: Guilty… sometimes, I suppose. Mostly I just feel odd about it, like, why did I buy all those sweets when I didn’t even really want them? I came in because my eating’s been all turned around for months now—some days I just pick at toast and coffee, and other days I’m standing in the bakery aisle buying things I don’t even enjoy. Then afterward I sort of sit there feeling foolish.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: I guess it feels like that sometimes, yes… not exactly like I’m hungry and choosing it. My eating’s just been all turned around for months. Some days I barely pick at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. I used to pass that aisle when my kids were little—well, not kids now, obviously—and it was just normal shopping. Now it feels like I’m just there doing it, like I can’t quite steer myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually buy or eat those sweets when you're feeling stressed, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s exactly stress. It’s more like… my eating’s just been all turned around for months now. Some days I’m just picking at toast and coffee, and then other days I end up in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. I used to have a better routine with meals, back when the house was busier, but now it’s all sort of… strange.\n\nDoctor: Do you eat those sweets to try to improve your mood?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’m doing it to cheer myself up, exactly. It’s more like my eating’s gotten all turned around for months now—some days it’s just toast and coffee, and then other days I’m standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. I used to be more normal about meals, I think… well, my mother always said breakfast mattered, but lately it’s just been strange.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure about the number, really. I don’t weigh myself much. It’s more that my eating has gotten strange for months… like some days I barely pick at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even want. I used to be more regular with meals, or I think I was.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not exactly. I mean, I don’t like how I look much, but I’m not sitting there counting every pound or anything. It’s more like food just feels… off. My sister used to worry about her jeans getting tight after holidays, and I remember thinking that was such a normal thing to care about. I don’t know if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, like within an hour?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, I guess. Not like… I don’t know what counts as “large,” but there are days I’ll go to the bakery aisle and buy sweets, and then I’m eating them even though I don’t really enjoy them. Other days I barely have toast and coffee. It’s all just been turned around for months now… I used to be more normal with meals, I think.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel guilty after eating?\n\nPatient: Guilty… sometimes, I suppose. Mostly I just feel odd about it, like, why did I buy all those sweets when I didn’t even really want them? I came in because my eating’s been all turned around for months now—some days I just pick at toast and coffee, and other days I’m standing in the bakery aisle buying things I don’t even enjoy. Then afterward I sort of sit there feeling foolish.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: I guess it feels like that sometimes, yes… not exactly like I’m hungry and choosing it. My eating’s just been all turned around for months. Some days I barely pick at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. I used to pass that aisle when my kids were little—well, not kids now, obviously—and it was just normal shopping. Now it feels like I’m just there doing it, like I can’t quite steer myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually buy or eat those sweets when you're feeling stressed, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s exactly stress. It’s more like… my eating’s just been all turned around for months now. Some days I’m just picking at toast and coffee, and then other days I end up in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. I used to have a better routine with meals, back when the house was busier, but now it’s all sort of… strange.\n\nDoctor: Do you eat those sweets to try to improve your mood?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’m doing it to cheer myself up, exactly. It’s more like my eating’s gotten all turned around for months now—some days it’s just toast and coffee, and then other days I’m standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. I used to be more sensible with shopping lists and all that… now I get home and wonder why I bought them.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually end up eating those sweets when you're feeling emotional?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure it’s always “emotional,” exactly. It’s more that my eating has just gotten all turned around for months. Some days I only pick at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m in that bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. I used to be better about regular meals… I don’t know, it just doesn’t feel normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself not interested in eating or not enjoying the taste of food?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, not always, but for months now food’s been sort of off for me. Some days I’m just picking at toast and drinking coffee, not really wanting anything. Then other days I end up in the bakery aisle buying sweets, and I don’t even enjoy them much when I have them. It’s strange, because I used to be more regular with meals.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're not interested in eating because you're just not hungry?\n\nPatient: I guess sometimes it feels like that, like I’m just not hungry, yeah. But it’s not very straightforward. I came in because my eating’s gotten all turned around for months now—some days I’m just picking at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. I don’t know why I do it exactly… I’ll go in for milk and end up staring at those little cakes, and then later I think, what was the point of that?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Maybe… I mean, if it was something I used to really like, I suppose I might eat some. But it’s hard to picture getting excited about it.

It’s like with other things too—I used to get all worked up over picking quilting squares, or bowling night, or planning the church potlucks, and now I’ll look at the fabric or the church calendar and just… nothing. I can’t make myself care the way I used to. So even favorite foods, I don’t know if they’d feel like they used to.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know if I’d say it’s filling a void exactly. It’s more like my eating has just gotten all mixed up for months. Some days I barely pick at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n19\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.8, F50.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… mostly my eating. It’s been all turned around for months now. Some days I just pick at a piece of toast and drink coffee, and then other days I’m standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. I don’t know why I do it. I used to be more normal about meals, I think… well, my mother always said breakfast mattered, but lately it’s just been strange.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure about the number, really. I don’t weigh myself much. It’s more that my eating has gotten strange for months… like some days I barely pick at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even want. I used to be more regular with meals, or I think I was.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not exactly. I mean, I don’t like how I look much, but I’m not sitting there counting every pound or anything. It’s more like food just feels… off. My sister used to worry about her jeans getting tight after holidays, and I remember thinking that was such a normal thing to care about. I don’t know if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, like within an hour?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, I guess. Not like… I don’t know what counts as “large,” but there are days I’ll go to the bakery aisle and buy sweets, and then I’m eating them even though I don’t really enjoy them. Other days I barely have toast and coffee. It’s all just been turned around for months now… I used to be more normal with meals, I think.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel guilty after eating?\n\nPatient: Guilty… sometimes, I suppose. Mostly I just feel odd about it, like, why did I buy all those sweets when I didn’t even really want them? I came in because my eating’s been all turned around for months now—some days I just pick at toast and coffee, and other days I’m standing in the bakery aisle buying things I don’t even enjoy. Then afterward I sort of sit there feeling foolish.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: I guess it feels like that sometimes, yes… not exactly like I’m hungry and choosing it. My eating’s just been all turned around for months. Some days I barely pick at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. I used to pass that aisle when my kids were little—well, not kids now, obviously—and it was just normal shopping. Now it feels like I’m just there doing it, like I can’t quite steer myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually buy or eat those sweets when you're feeling stressed, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s exactly stress. It’s more like… my eating’s just been all turned around for months now. Some days I’m just picking at toast and coffee, and then other days I end up in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. I used to have a better routine with meals, back when the house was busier, but now it’s all sort of… strange.\n\nDoctor: Do you eat those sweets to try to improve your mood?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’m doing it to cheer myself up, exactly. It’s more like my eating’s gotten all turned around for months now—some days it’s just toast and coffee, and then other days I’m standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. I used to be more sensible with shopping lists and all that… now I get home and wonder why I bought them.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually end up eating those sweets when you're feeling emotional?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure it’s always “emotional,” exactly. It’s more that my eating has just gotten all turned around for months. Some days I only pick at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m in that bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. I used to be better about regular meals… I don’t know, it just doesn’t feel normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself not interested in eating or not enjoying the taste of food?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, not always, but for months now food’s been sort of off for me. Some days I’m just picking at toast and drinking coffee, not really wanting anything. Then other days I end up in the bakery aisle buying sweets, and I don’t even enjoy them much when I have them. It’s strange, because I used to be more regular with meals.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're not interested in eating because you're just not hungry?\n\nPatient: I guess sometimes it feels like that, like I’m just not hungry, yeah. But it’s not very straightforward. I came in because my eating’s gotten all turned around for months now—some days I’m just picking at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. I don’t know why I do it exactly… I’ll go in for milk and end up staring at those little cakes, and then later I think, what was the point of that?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your lack of interest in eating is related to how you're feeling about yourself or your life?\n\nPatient: Maybe… I don’t know. It’s hard to separate it out. I came in because my eating’s been all turned around for months now—some days I’m just picking at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. I used to pass that aisle without even thinking about it, you know, and now I’ll just stand there like I forgot what I came in for. So… maybe it is tied to how I feel about things, but mostly I just know it doesn’t feel like me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really kept track on a scale, to be honest. My eating’s just been… off, for months. Some days I’ll only pick at toast and drink coffee, and then other days I’m in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even enjoy. So I don’t know exactly about the weight, but the way I’m eating has definitely changed.\n\nDoctor: Are you ever afraid of eating because you think it will make you gain weight?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s really fear of gaining weight, no… it’s more like my eating’s just gotten all turned around. Some days I barely get through toast and coffee, and then other days I end up in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even enjoy. It’s strange, because I’ll stand there looking at them like they matter, and then once I have them I don’t even know why I bought them.\n\nDoctor: Do you often avoid eating with other people?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I don’t really think of it as avoiding people exactly. I don’t have a lot of meals with other people these days anyway, so it’s hard to say. Sometimes it feels easier to just eat whatever, whenever, without anyone asking about it. But I don’t know if that’s “avoid,” you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would eat more normally if you didn't worry about your weight at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t think the weight part is really what’s driving it. If I didn’t worry about weight at all, I’m not sure it would suddenly go back to normal. It’s more that my eating has been all turned around for months—some days I just pick at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. I’ll get home and think, why did I even get these?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating because you can't stop yourself, even when you want to?\n\nPatient: Sometimes it feels a little like that, but not exactly like I can’t stop. It’s more that my eating’s been all turned around for months now. Some days I barely want anything, just toast and coffee maybe, and then other days I find myself in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. I’ll get home and sort of stare at them like… why did I do that? It doesn’t feel normal, but I don’t know if I’d call it being out of control.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would enjoy eating if you were presented with your favorite foods?\n\nPatient: Maybe… I mean, if it was something I used to really like, I suppose I might eat some.
